Employees & culture


at Kaspi.kz


We understand that to continue developing

innovative products for our customers and

generate value for all our stakeholders, we must

attract, develop and retain the best possible talent.

Values embedded in our culture including team-

work, integrity, continued learning, innovation and

accountability are key to our efforts to develop our

human capital and long-term success.




Our commitment to our employees has never

been tested more, than over the last couple of

years. The Covid-19 pandemic and more recently

civil unrest in Kazakhstan led to unprecedented

demands on our employees personal and

professional lives. We remained steadfast in our

commitment to our employees, taking steps to

prioritise their safety, health and wellbeing

throughout.




In 2021 we also opened new state of the art office

facilities at our head office in Almaty. We aim to

create one of the best places to work and modern

office space and the related wow factor that

comes from this are just one aspect of this

strategy. Our new office facilities include the latest

environmental management systems.

Management is responsible for the other information. The other information comprises the information            Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial statements

report.                                                                                                         that an audit conducted in accordance with ISAs will always detect a material misstatement when it exists.
                                                                                                                Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in the aggregate,
Our opinion on the consolidated financial statements does not cover the other information and we will not       they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of these
express any form of assurance conclusion thereon.                                                               consolidated financial statements.

                                                                                                           Basis of accounting
2. Basis of presentation
                                                                                                           These consolidated financial statements have been prepared in accordance with International
Foreign currency translation                                                                               Financial Reporting Standards (“IFRS”) as issued by the International Accounting Standards Board.

For the purpose of the consolidated financial statements, the results and financial position of each       The Group and its subsidiaries maintain their accounting records in accordance with IFRS. The
group company are expressed in Kazakhstani tenge which is the functional currency of the Group,            consolidated financial statements have been prepared on the historical cost basis, except for the
and the presentation currency for the consolidated financial statements.                                   revaluation of certain properties and financial instruments that are measured at revalued amounts
                                                                                                           or fair values at the end of each reporting period, as explained in the accounting policies below. The
The individual financial statements of each group company are presented in the currency of the             Group presents its statement of financial position in order of liquidity.
primary economic environment in which it operates (its functional currency). In preparing the
financial statements of each individual entity, monetary assets and liabilities denominated in             Offsetting
currencies other than the entity’s functional currency (foreign currencies) are translated at the
appropriate spot rates or exchange rates prevailing at the reporting date. Transactions in foreign         Financial assets and financial liabilities are offset and the net amount reported in the consolidated
currencies are initially recorded at their spot rates at the date of the transaction.                      statement of financial position only when there is a legally enforceable right to offset the recognised
                                                                                                           amounts and there is an intention to settle on a net basis, or to realise the assets and settle the
                                                                                                           liability simultaneously. Income and expense is not offset in the consolidated statement of profit or
                                                                                                           loss unless required or permitted by any accounting standard or interpretation, and as specifically
                                                                                                           disclosed in the accounting policies of the Group.

Financial assets                                                                                                         Expected credit loss (“ECL”) measurement – definitions

All recognised financial assets that are within the scope of IFRS 9 are required to be measured                          ECL is a probability-weighted measurement of the present value of future cash shortfalls
subsequently at amortised cost or fair value on the basis of the entity’s business model for managing the                (i.e., the weighted average of credit losses, with the respective risks of default occurring in a given
financial assets and the contractual cash flow characteristics of the financial assets.                                  time period used as weights). An ECL measurement is unbiased and should be determined by
                                                                                                                         evaluating a range of possible outcomes.
Under IFRS 9, all debt financial assets that do not meet a “solely payment of principal and interest” (“SPPI”)
criterion, are classified at initial recognition as fair value through profit or loss (“FVTPL”). Under this criterion,   An ECL measurement is based on four components used by the Group:
debt instruments that do not correspond to a “basic lending arrangement”, are measured at FVTPL. For
                                                                s
debt financial assets that meet the SPPI criterion, classification at initial recognition is determined based on            •    Exposure at Default (“EAD”) – an estimate of exposure at a future default date, taking into
the business model under which these instruments are managed:                                                                    account expected changes in exposure after the reporting date, including repayments of
                                                                                                                                 principal and interest, and expected drawdowns on committed facilities.
   •    Financial assets, other than equity investments, that are managed on a “hold to collect” basis are                  •    Probability of Default (“PD”) – an estimate of the likelihood of default to occur over a given
        measured at amortised cost;                                                                                              time period.
   •    Financial assets, other than equity investments, that are managed on a “hold to collect and for sale”               •    Loss Given Default (“LGD”) – an estimate of a loss arising on default. It is based on the
        basis are measured at fair value through other comprehensive income (“FVTOCI”);                                          difference between contractual cash flows due and those that the lender would expect to
   •    Financial assets, including equity investments, that are managed on another basis, including trading                     receive, including from any collateral. It usually expressed as a percentage of EAD.
        financial assets, will be measured at FVTPL.                                                                        •    Discount Rate – a tool to discount an expected loss from the present value at the reporting
                                                                                                                                 date. The discount rate represents the effective interest rate (“EIR”) for the financial

During the recovery period, such modified loans are classified to Stage 3, with corresponding increase in        profit or loss. A cumulative gain or loss that had been recognised in other comprehensive income is
loss allowance. After the recovery period, such modified loans are allocated to the relevant impairment          allocated between the part that continues to be recognised and the part that is no longer recognised
category, based on its days past due and impairment methodology.                                                 on the basis of the relative fair values of those parts.

                                                                                                                 Financial liabilities

                                                                                                                 Financial liabilities, such as due to banks, customer accounts, debt securities issued, subordinated
                                                                                                                 debt and other financial liabilities are initially recognised at fair value. Subsequently amounts due are
                                                                                                                 stated at amortised cost and any difference between carrying and redemption value is recognised
                                                                                                                 in the consolidated statement of profit or loss over the period of the borrowings using the effective
                                                                                                                 interest method as a component of interest expense.

21. Share-based compensation reserve                                                                              Phantom share expenses in the amount of KZT 426 million refer to revaluation of the 1st year of the previous
                                                                                                                  Phantom share program vested in the 1st quarter of 2021.
In the fourth quarter of 2020, the Group introduced share-based compensation, according to which senior
executives and other core Group personnel receive share options or phantom shares of the Group. Awards            Share Options
are payable in annual installments over a five year vesting schedule for share options and a two year vesting
schedule for phantom shares.                                                                                      Share options are measured at fair value of the shares at the date of grant using the Black Scholes model.
                                                                                                                  The fair value determined at the grant date is expensed over the five-year vesting period, based on the group’s
In the third quarter of 2021, the Group replaced its phantom shares program with share options to better          estimate of the number of shares that will eventually vest. Recipients of Share Options are entitled to the
align the interest of the participants with the long-term interests of the Company and its Shareholders. As a     dividends once shares vested and exercised.
result of such modification, a liability for phantom shares as at the date of replacement was derecognised
and share options measured at their fair value at the date of the replacement were recognised. Awards are         The following table summarizes the details of the share options outstanding:
payable in annual installments over a five-year vesting schedule. The previous phantom share program had
a two-year vesting schedule. Now all Share-based programs of the group are equity settled with no phantom                                                                                          Year ended       Year ended
shares involved.                                                                                                                                                                                 31 December      31 December

26. Risk management policy                                                                                             Collateral held as security and other credit enhancements

The Group permanently advances it’s risk management environment, to fit up-to-date challenges and risks the            The Group holds collateral or other credit enhancements to mitigate credit risk associated with financial
Group is exposed to. The Group is exposed to the following types of risks: credit risk, liquidity risk, market risk.   assets. The main types of collateral obtained are as follows:

Credit risk                                                                                                            •    For reverse repurchase transactions – securities;
                                                                                                                       •    For commercial lending – charges over real estate properties and vehicles.
The Group is exposed to credit risk, which is the risk that a customer will be unable to pay amounts in full
when due. The Group’s credit risk exposure arises primarily from our consumer finance business through the             Although, the Group uses collateral as credit enhancement to mitigate its exposure to credit risk, major part
Fintech Platform. To manage credit risk during loan origination the Group centralized all processes related            of its loan portfolio is represented by unsecured loans. Thus, as at 31 December 2021 and 2020, unsecured
to decision making, verification and accounting through it’s headquarters. The Group has developed an                  gross carrying amount of loans to customers were KZT 1,989,342 million and KZT 1,162,426 million,
automated, centralised and big data-driven proprietary loan approval process that enables it to make instant           respectively.
credit decisions. The risk management block is responsible for maintaining scoring models and decision-
making process. The quality of approved loans are monitored by risk management block on day-to-day basis               As at 31 December 2021 and 2020, credit impaired loans with a net carrying value of KZT 16,084 million
with periodical validation of the models.                                                                              and KZT 19,330 million, respectively were either fully or partially collateralized, reflecting the extent to which
                                                                                                                       collateral and other credit enhancements mitigate credit risk.

Terminology                             Definition




Delinquency Rate                        The share of loans that were not delinquent in the previous month but

                                        missed their current due date payment



FPD (First Payment                      The share of loans with the missed first payment

Default)



GMV (Gross Merchandise                  The total value of goods and services sold across our Marketplace Platform

Value)                                  during the respective period



Loss Rate Vintages                      Expected loss rate of portfolio originated in specific quarter/month as a

                                        combination of actual NPL as of reporting date and expected


                                        recovery of NPL based on statistics



MAU (Monthly Active                     The monthly number of users with at least one discrete session (visit) in

Users)                                  excess of 10 seconds on the Mobile App in the last calendar month of each

                                        respective period; average MAU is a simple average of MAU for any given

                                        quarter



Net Income Margin                       The ratio of Net Income to Revenue



RTPV (Revenue-generating                TPV minus non revenue generating P2P Payments within the Kaspi

TPV)                                    Ecosystem



SPD (Second Payment                     The share of loans with the missed first and second payments

Default)



Take Rate                               For Payments Platform: ratio of transaction and membership revenue

                                        (excluding Kaspi Business) to RTPV


                                        For Marketplace Platform: ratio of seller fees to GMV



TFV (Total Finance Value)               The total value of loans to consumers issued within the Fintech Platform

Conversion Rate                         Platform
